Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more, but... um, I guess I’m here because I’ve been having some thoughts lately that are a little embarrassing to admit.

But sometimes on my drive home from the school—I teach secondary school—I just start wondering if my family and my students would actually be better off without me.

Um... well, I guess it’s been a little while now. I really hope I didn't wait too long to say something, I just kept hoping it would pass on its own. I think it started maybe... gosh, about a year and seven months ago? Give or take?

Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I feel terrible even admitting this, because I know I should be making more of an effort. I used to really look forward to my softball league, and my book club... and I usually love working in my garden. Plus, I help run the robotics club at school, which is usually so fun.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah. I guess I do. I feel awful even saying it out loud because I know I should be more resilient, and I really try to be, but... yes. 

Like, if a lesson goes badly at school, or even if just one student looks confused, I just... I take it so hard. I take it as proof that I’m letting everyone down. I'll just keep replaying the moment in my head over and over, basically convincing myself that I’m a terrible teacher... and honestly, a terrible mother, too. 

I know I shouldn't think like that, and I promise I'm trying to be better about it. I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself. I know people have real problems. Is it... do you think it's normal to be that hard on yourself when you're just really stressed out? I hope it's okay that I shared that.

Um... well, if I'm being completely honest... I guess it's been going on for a while now. Probably about... a year and seven months?

Um... well, I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I still go to things, mostly. I really don't want to seem like I'm just giving up on my commitments.

I feel so guilty about it, especially with the kids in robotics, because I know they rely on me. I promise I'm trying to snap out of it and get back to being my normal self. Is it... is it normal to feel that way when you're just really overwhelmed with work? I hope I'm explaining this right.

I really hope I didn't wait too long to say something, I just kept hoping it would pass on its own. I think it started maybe... gosh, about a year and seven months ago? Give or take? \n\nThat was right around the time I agreed to take over the STEM assessment redesign at school, which was on top of my regular teaching load. I just assumed I was being overtired, you know? Like, anyone would be exhausted with that extra work, and I really didn't want to complain or let my principal down. So I just kind of brushed it off and tried to push through. \n\nIs it... does it make sense that taking on extra work would trigger thoughts like that? I just really hope I'm not overreacting by bringing all this up.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I feel terrible even admitting this, because I know I should be making more of an effort. I used to really look forward to my softball league, and my book club... and I usually love working in my garden. Plus, I help run the robotics club at school, which is usually so fun. \n\nBut lately, I... I don't know, I just find myself mostly going through the motions with all of it. Actually, I've been canceling on them a lot recently, which I feel so incredibly guilty about because I hate letting my friends or the kids down. It's just that none of it really feels enjoyable right now? It just feels like another obligation I have to push through. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful and ungrateful. Is that... do you think that's a normal thing to happen when you're just really overwhelmed with work? I really hope I'm not just becoming a lazy person.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess maybe a little bit? It's just... \n\nWell, most school days, I kind of just... forget to eat? Or I guess I just avoid it until the late afternoon. I know that's probably a terrible habit, and I really should be taking better care of myself, especially with the kids watching me. It's just that with the STEM redesign and everything else, stopping for lunch just feels like... too much effort sometimes? \n\nI know I shouldn't be skipping meals, and I feel really silly even bringing it up. Is it... is it okay that I mentioned that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that you think you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess I do. I feel awful even saying it out loud because I know I should be more resilient, and I really try to be, but... yes. \n\nLike, if a lesson goes badly at school, or even if just one student looks confused, I just... I take it so hard. I take it as proof that I’m letting everyone down. I'll just keep replaying the moment in my head over and over, basically convincing myself that I’m a terrible teacher... and honestly, a terrible mother, too. \n\nI know I shouldn't think like that, and I promise I'm trying to be better about it. I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself. I know people have real problems. I try to just stay focused and keep things moving normally. I hope that's okay? I don't want to be one of those people who is always complaining about being stressed out, you know? I feel like I handle things pretty well, for the most part. \n\nDoes that make sense? Um... a whole two weeks? I don't... I don't think so. 

I mean, I definitely have days where I feel a bit down, or where I just don't have that spark for things, like I was saying before. But I really try not to let it drag on like that. Um... two years? No, definitely not that long. I wouldn't let things go on for two whole years without trying to fix it, I promise.

I feel so bad even pointing to that, because I really was so honored that the administration trusted me with such a big project, and I know I should be able to handle it all without getting so down on myself. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about the extra work. It's just... I guess that's when the guilt of feeling like I wasn't doing enough for anybody really started to pile up. \n\nDoes that make sense? I'm really trying to get it back, I promise.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of two weeks or more where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 33 / secondary_school_teacher / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I think you asked me this earlier, didn't you? Or something similar? I'm sorry if I wasn't clear before, I really don't want to be confusing. 

Like I said, I definitely don't think I've ever had a full two weeks where I felt completely depressed or lost interest in everything. I would never let myself go that long without snapping out of it. I have responsibilities to my students and my colleagues, and I try so hard to be reliable for them. 

I mean, I definitely have rough days, and like I mentioned, the things I usually enjoy haven't been as fun lately. But I still show up, I still teach, I still try to be positive. I wouldn't let a bad mood stretch on for two whole weeks straight. That just wouldn't be fair to anyone. 

I hope I'm explaining this right. I'm really trying to give you the best answers I can. Am I... am I making sense?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, no, I definitely haven't done anything to hurt myself. I would never do that, I promise. I know how terrible that would be for everyone around me, especially my students. 

But... I guess I should be completely honest, since that's why I'm here. I... I have been catching myself having these thoughts lately. Like... just thinking it would be so much easier if I just didn't wake up in the morning. And sometimes, when I'm driving home from school, I... I start wondering if my family and my students would just be better off without me. 

It really scares me when my brain goes there, because I don't want to actually do anything! I really don't. It's just... these thoughts kind of pop up, and I try to push them away as fast as I can. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible person, or like I'm giving up. I'm really not. I just... I wanted to make sure I told you everything so I can get back on track. Is it... is it normal to have thoughts like that when you're feeling so run down?

Um, well... again, definitely not for two years. I just wanted to make sure I was clear on that! I really try my best not to let things get that bad. 

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest about how things have been lately... I have been struggling a little bit with enjoying things.
